Derived Blueprint — aml-program-rules-refactor-demo

Mode: refactor · 42 sources · 411 obligations · 307 definitions · rendered 2026-07-20 14:37 UTC
Ratified scope. Distill the current federal AML/CFT program-establishment obligations (the program/'pillars' rules) for U.S. depository institutions, money services businesses, and broker-dealers (including the FINRA SRO layer) under FinCEN, the federal banking agencies, and the SEC as of a pinned 2026 date into a derived blueprint, defect register, and refactored target blueprint with operation trace for the Foundry working group, using statutes, regulations, SRO rules, official guidance, and examination manuals as authority and enforcement actions as evidence only, treating other covered sectors (casinos, mutual funds, insurance, etc.), OFAC/sanctions, fraud, and state AML/licensing as boundary regimes and SAR/CTR/CDD operational duties as coordination layer.
This rendering is DERIVED from the structured store (one extraction file per source, citation quotes machine-verified). Per the fidelity rule (P2.3) it preserves the corpus as built — contradictions, duplication and divergent definitions included; resolving them is Phase 3 work, recorded as operations, never edits here.

ffiec-man-cp-01

Assessing the BSA/AML Compliance Program · AssessingTheBSAAMLComplianceProgram/01
Actor / modalityObligation
mustBanksestablish and maintain procedures reasonably designed to assure and monitor compliance with BSA regulatory requirements (BSA/AML compliance program)
✓ “Banks must establish and maintain procedures reasonably designed to assure and monitor compliance with BSA regulatory requirements (BSA/AML compliance program).”
mustBank (via the BSA/AML compliance program)have the BSA/AML compliance program be written, approved by the board of directors, and noted in the board minutes
✓ “The BSA/AML compliance program[10] must be written, approved by the board of directors,[11] and noted in the board minutes.”
shouldBank (via the BSA/AML compliance program)make the BSA/AML compliance program commensurate with the bank's ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risk profile
trigger: To achieve the purposes of the BSA
✓ “To achieve the purposes of the BSA, the BSA/AML compliance program should be commensurate with the bank's ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risk profile.”
shouldBank (policies, procedures, processes, and practices)align policies, procedures, processes, and practices with the bank's unique ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risk profile
✓ “policies, procedures, processes, and practices should align with the bank's unique ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risk profile.”
mustBank (via the BSA/AML compliance program)provide for a system of internal controls to assure ongoing compliance
✓ “The BSA/AML compliance program must provide for the following requirements:[12] A system of internal controls to assure ongoing compliance.”
mustBank (via the BSA/AML compliance program)provide for independent testing for compliance to be conducted by bank personnel or by an outside party
✓ “Independent testing for compliance to be conducted by bank personnel or by an outside party.”
mustBank (via the BSA/AML compliance program)provide for designation of an individual or individuals responsible for coordinating and monitoring day-to-day compliance (BSA compliance officer)
✓ “Designation of an individual or individuals responsible for coordinating and monitoring day-to-day compliance (BSA compliance officer).”
mustBank (via the BSA/AML compliance program)provide for training for appropriate personnel
✓ “Training for appropriate personnel.”
mustBank (via the BSA/AML compliance program)include a customer identification program (CIP) with risk-based procedures that enable the bank to form a reasonable belief that it knows the true identity of its customers
✓ “the BSA/AML compliance program must include a customer identification program (CIP) with risk-based procedures that enable the bank to form a reasonable belief that it knows the true identity of its customers.”
mustBank (via the BSA/AML compliance program)include appropriate risk-based procedures for conducting ongoing customer due diligence (CDD) and complying with beneficial ownership requirements for legal entity customers as set forth in regulations issued by FinCEN
✓ “The BSA/AML compliance program must also include appropriate risk-based procedures for conducting ongoing customer due diligence (CDD) and complying with beneficial ownership requirements for legal entity customers as set forth in regulations issued by Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN).”
shouldExaminersconsider all pertinent information when assessing the adequacy of the bank's BSA/AML compliance program
✓ “The assessment of the adequacy of the bank's BSA/AML compliance program is bank-specific, and examiners should consider all pertinent information.”
shouldExaminers / bank managementdiscuss BSA/AML examination findings with relevant bank management
✓ “BSA/AML examination findings should be discussed with relevant bank management”
mustExaminersinclude findings in the report of examination (ROE) or supervisory correspondence
✓ “findings must be included in the report of examination (ROE) or supervisory correspondence.”
shouldExaminersdevelop and document a preliminary assessment of the bank's program
trigger: Once examiners complete the review of the bank's BSA/AML compliance program
✓ “Once examiners complete the review of the bank's BSA/AML compliance program, they should develop and document a preliminary assessment of the bank's program.”
shouldExaminersrevisit the initial BSA/AML examination plan to determine whether additional areas of review are necessary to assess the adequacy of the bank's BSA/AML compliance program, relative to its risk profile, and the bank's compliance with BSA regulatory requirements
trigger: At this point (after completing review)
✓ “At this point, examiners should revisit the initial BSA/AML examination plan to determine whether additional areas of review are necessary to assess the adequacy of the bank's BSA/AML compliance program, relative to its risk profile, and the bank's compliance with BSA regulatory requirements.”
shouldExaminersdocument and support any changes to the examination plan, if necessary, then proceed to the applicable examination and testing procedures
✓ “Examiners should document and support any changes to the examination plan, if necessary, then proceed to the applicable examination and testing procedures in Assessing Compliance with BSA Regulatory Requirements, Risks Associated with Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing, and Office of Foreign Assets Control.”
shouldExaminersproceed to Developing Conclusions and Finalizing the Examination
trigger: Once all relevant examination and testing procedures are completed as documented in the examination plan
✓ “Once all relevant examination and testing procedures are completed as documented in the examination plan, examiners should proceed to Developing Conclusions and Finalizing the Examination.”

ffiec-man-cp-02

BSA/AML Internal Controls · AssessingTheBSAAMLComplianceProgram/02
Actor / modalityObligation
mustboard of directors, acting through senior managementensure that the bank maintains a system of internal controls to assure ongoing compliance with BSA regulatory requirements
✓ “The board of directors, acting through senior management, is ultimately responsible for ensuring that the bank maintains a system of internal controls to assure ongoing compliance with BSA regulatory requirements.”
shouldbank's system of internal controls (level and type)be commensurate with the bank's size or complexity, and organizational structure
✓ “The system of internal controls, including the level and type, should be commensurate with the bank's size or complexity, and organizational structure.”
shouldexaminersdetermine whether the bank's internal controls are designed to assure ongoing compliance with BSA regulatory requirements and incorporate the risk assessment, provide program continuity, facilitate IT oversight, provide timely regulatory updates, incorporate dual controls/segregation of duties, include mechanisms to inform the board and senior management, and establish specific BSA compliance responsibilities
✓ “Examiners should determine whether the bank's internal controls are designed to assure ongoing compliance with BSA regulatory requirements and:”
shouldemployees who complete the reporting forms (such as SARs, CTRs, and CTR exemptions)generally should not also be responsible for the decision to file the reports or grant the exemptions
✓ “For example, employees who complete the reporting forms (such as suspicious activity reports (SARs), currency transaction reports (CTRs), and CTR exemptions) generally should not also be responsible for the decision to file the reports or grant the exemptions.”
shouldbank (this list of internal control features)be tailored to reflect the bank's ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risk profile
✓ “This list is not all-inclusive and should be tailored to reflect the bank's ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risk profile.”
shouldexaminersdetermine whether the bank's system of internal controls is designed to mitigate and manage the ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks, and comply with BSA regulatory requirements
✓ “Examiners should determine whether the bank's system of internal controls is designed to mitigate and manage the ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks, and comply with BSA regulatory requirements.”
shouldexaminersassess the adequacy of internal controls based on the factors listed above
✓ “Examiners should assess the adequacy of internal controls based on the factors listed above.”
TermDefinition (as given)
Internal controlsthe bank's policies, procedures, and processes designed to mitigate and manage ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks and to achieve compliance with BSA regulatory requirements
✓ “Internal controls are the bank's policies, procedures, and processes designed to mitigate and manage ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks and to achieve compliance with BSA regulatory requirements.”
Departmental internal controlscontrols that typically address risks and compliance requirements unique to a particular line of business or department and are part of a comprehensive, bank-wide BSA/AML compliance program
✓ “Departmental internal controls typically address risks and compliance requirements unique to a particular line of business or department and are part of a comprehensive, bank-wide BSA/AML compliance program.”
BSA regulatory requirements(undefined here)
✓ “Assess the bank's system of internal controls to assure ongoing compliance with BSA regulatory requirements.”
ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks(undefined here)
✓ “designed to mitigate and manage ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks”
SARs (suspicious activity reports)(undefined here)
✓ “such as suspicious activity reports (SARs), currency transaction reports (CTRs), and CTR exemptions”
CTRs (currency transaction reports)(undefined here)
✓ “such as suspicious activity reports (SARs), currency transaction reports (CTRs), and CTR exemptions”
BSA/AML risk assessment(undefined here)
✓ “Incorporate the bank's BSA/AML risk assessment and the identification of ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks, along with any changes in those risks.”

ffiec-man-cp-03

BSA/AML Independent Testing · AssessingTheBSAAMLComplianceProgram/03
Actor / modalityObligation
shouldbank (independent testing)have independent testing conducted by the internal audit department, outside auditors, consultants, or other qualified independent parties
✓ “Independent testing should be conducted by the internal audit department, outside auditors, consultants, or other qualified independent parties.”
maybanks that do not employ outside auditors or consultants or do not have internal audit departmentscomply with the independent testing requirement by using qualified bank staff who are not involved in the function being tested
trigger: bank does not employ outside auditors or consultants or does not have internal audit departments
✓ “Banks that do not employ outside auditors or consultants or do not have internal audit departments may comply with this requirement by using qualified bank staff who are not involved in the function being tested.”
shouldbanks engaging outside auditors or consultantsensure that the persons conducting the BSA/AML independent testing are not involved in other BSA-related functions at the bank that may present a conflict of interest or lack of independence, such as training or developing policies and procedures
trigger: engaging outside auditors or consultants
✓ “Banks engaging outside auditors or consultants should ensure that the persons conducting the BSA/AML independent testing are not involved in other BSA-related functions at the bank that may present a conflict of interest or lack of independence, such as training or developing policies and procedures.”
shouldparty conducting the BSA/AML independent testingreport directly to the board of directors or to a designated board committee comprised primarily, or completely, of outside directors
✓ “Regardless of who performs the independent testing, the party conducting the BSA/AML independent testing should report directly to the board of directors or to a designated board committee comprised primarily, or completely, of outside directors.”
maybanks with a community focus, less complex operations, and lower-risk profiles for ML/TF and other illicit financial activitiesconsider utilizing a shared resource as part of a collaborative arrangement to conduct independent testing
trigger: community focus, less complex operations, and lower-risk profiles
✓ “Banks with a community focus, less complex operations, and lower-risk profiles for ML/TF and other illicit financial activities may consider utilizing a shared resource as part of a collaborative arrangement to conduct independent testing.”
shouldbank (independent testing)have independent testing, including the frequency, commensurate with the ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risk profile of the bank and the bank's overall risk management strategy
✓ “There is no regulatory requirement establishing BSA/AML independent testing frequency. Independent testing, including the frequency, should be commensurate with the ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risk profile of the bank and the bank's overall risk management strategy.”
maybankconduct independent testing over periodic intervals (for example, every 12-18 months) and/or when there are significant changes in the bank's risk profile, systems, compliance staff, or processes
trigger: periodic intervals and/or significant changes in the bank's risk profile, systems, compliance staff, or processes
threshold: for example, every 12-18 months
✓ “The bank may conduct independent testing over periodic intervals (for example, every 12-18 months) and/or when there are significant changes in the bank's risk profile, systems, compliance staff, or processes.”
shouldbank (independent testing of specific BSA requirements)be risk-based and evaluate the quality of risk management related to ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks for significant banking operations across the organization
✓ “Independent testing of specific BSA requirements should be risk-based and evaluate the quality of risk management related to ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks for significant banking operations across the organization.”
shouldbank (risk-based independent testing)include evaluating pertinent internal controls and information technology sources, systems, and processes used to support the BSA/AML compliance program
✓ “Risk-based independent testing should include evaluating pertinent internal controls and information technology sources, systems, and processes used to support the BSA/AML compliance program.”
shouldbank (independent testing)evaluate the overall adequacy of the bank's BSA/AML compliance program and the bank's compliance with BSA regulatory requirements
✓ “The independent testing should evaluate the overall adequacy of the bank's BSA/AML compliance program and the bank's compliance with BSA regulatory requirements.”
shouldbank (independent testing)contain sufficient information for the reviewer to reach a conclusion about the overall adequacy of the BSA/AML compliance program
threshold: At a minimum
✓ “At a minimum, the independent testing should contain sufficient information for the reviewer (e.g., board of directors, senior management, BSA compliance officer, review auditor, or an examiner) to reach a conclusion about the overall adequacy of the BSA/AML compliance program.”
shouldauditorsdocument the independent testing scope, procedures performed, transaction testing completed, and any findings
✓ “Auditors should document the independent testing scope, procedures performed, transaction testing completed, and any findings.”
shouldbank (independent testing documentation)make all independent testing documentation and supporting workpapers available for examiner review
✓ “All independent testing documentation and supporting workpapers should be available for examiner review.”
shouldbankdocument and report violations; exceptions to bank policies, procedures, or processes; or other deficiencies noted during the independent testing to the board of directors or a designated board committee in a timely manner
trigger: violations, exceptions, or other deficiencies noted during independent testing
✓ “Violations; exceptions to bank policies, procedures, or processes; or other deficiencies noted during the independent testing should be documented and reported to the board of directors or a designated board committee in a timely manner.”
shouldboard of directors, or a designated board committee, and appropriate stafftrack deficiencies and document progress implementing corrective actions
✓ “The board of directors, or a designated board committee, and appropriate staff should track deficiencies and document progress implementing corrective actions.”
shouldexaminersreview relevant documents such as the auditor's report(s), scope, and supporting workpapers, as needed
✓ “Examiners should review relevant documents such as the auditor's report(s), scope, and supporting workpapers, as needed.”
shouldexaminersdetermine whether there is an explicit statement in the report(s) about the bank's overall compliance with BSA regulatory requirements or, at a minimum, sufficient information to reach a conclusion about the overall adequacy of the BSA/AML compliance program
✓ “Examiners should determine whether there is an explicit statement in the report(s) about the bank's overall compliance with BSA regulatory requirements or, at a minimum, sufficient information to reach a conclusion about the overall adequacy of the BSA/AML compliance program.”
shouldexaminersdetermine whether the testing was conducted in an independent manner
✓ “Examiners should determine whether the testing was conducted in an independent manner.”
mayexaminersevaluate, as applicable, the subject matter expertise, qualifications, and independence of the person or persons performing the independent testing
✓ “Examiners may also evaluate, as applicable,[16] the subject matter expertise, qualifications, and independence of the person or persons performing the independent testing.”
shouldexaminersdetermine whether the independent testing sufficiently covers ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks within the bank's operations and whether the frequency is commensurate with the bank's risk profile
✓ “Examiners should determine whether the independent testing sufficiently covers ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks within the bank's operations and whether the frequency is commensurate with the bank's risk profile.”
shouldexaminersreview whether violations; exceptions to policies, procedures, or processes; or other deficiencies are reported to the board of directors or a designated board committee in a timely manner, whether they are tracked, and whether corrective actions are documented
✓ “Examiners should also review whether violations; exceptions to policies, procedures, or processes; or other deficiencies are reported to the board of directors or a designated board committee in a timely manner, whether they are tracked, and whether corrective actions are documented.”

ffiec-man-cp-04

BSA Compliance Officer · AssessingTheBSAAMLComplianceProgram/04
Actor / modalityObligation
mustthe bank's board of directorsdesignate a qualified individual or individuals to serve as the BSA compliance officer
✓ “The bank's board of directors must designate a qualified individual or individuals to serve as the BSA compliance officer.[18]”
shouldthe board of directorsprovide oversight for senior management and the BSA compliance officer in the implementation of the bank's board-approved BSA/AML compliance program
✓ “The board of directors is ultimately responsible for the bank's BSA/AML compliance and should provide oversight for senior management and the BSA compliance officer in the implementation of the bank's board-approved BSA/AML compliance program.[19]”
mustthe board of directorsensure that the BSA compliance officer has appropriate authority, independence, and access to resources to administer an adequate BSA/AML compliance program based on the bank's ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risk profile
✓ “The board of directors is responsible for ensuring that the BSA compliance officer has appropriate authority, independence, and access to resources to administer an adequate BSA/AML compliance program based on the bank's ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risk profile.”
shouldthe BSA compliance officerregularly report the status of ongoing compliance with the BSA to the board of directors and senior management so that they can make informed decisions about existing risk exposure and the overall BSA/AML compliance program
✓ “The BSA compliance officer should regularly report the status of ongoing compliance with the BSA to the board of directors and senior management so that they can make informed decisions about existing risk exposure and the overall BSA/AML compliance program.”
shouldthe BSA compliance officer (in reporting to the board of directors or a designated board committee)include pertinent BSA-related information, including the required notification of suspicious activity report (SAR) filings, in reporting about the status of ongoing compliance
trigger: reporting to the board of directors or a designated board committee about the status of ongoing compliance
✓ “Reporting to the board of directors or a designated board committee about the status of ongoing compliance should include pertinent BSA-related information, including the required notification of suspicious activity report (SAR) filings.”
maythe BSA compliance officerdelegate BSA/AML duties to staff, while remaining responsible for overseeing the day-to-day BSA/AML compliance program
✓ “The BSA compliance officer may delegate BSA/AML duties to staff, but the officer is responsible for overseeing the day-to-day BSA/AML compliance program.”
shouldthe BSA compliance officerbe competent, as demonstrated by knowledge of the BSA and related regulations, implementation of the bank's BSA/AML compliance program, and understanding of the bank's ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risk profile associated with its banking activities
✓ “The BSA compliance officer should be competent, as demonstrated by knowledge of the BSA and related regulations, implementation of the bank's BSA/AML compliance program, and understanding of the bank's ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risk profile associated with its banking activities.”
shouldthe BSA compliance officerhave access to suitable resources, which may include adequate staffing with the necessary skills and expertise and systems to support the timely identification, measurement, monitoring, reporting, and management of the bank's ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks
✓ “The BSA compliance officer should have access to suitable resources.”
✓ “adequate staffing with the skills and expertise necessary for the bank's overall risk level (based on products, services, customers, and geographic locations), size or complexity, and organizational structure; and systems to support the timely identification, measurement, monitoring, reporting, and management of the bank's ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks.”
shouldexaminersconfirm that the bank's board of directors has designated an individual or individuals responsible for the overall BSA/AML compliance program who are appropriately qualified
✓ “Examiners should confirm that the bank's board of directors has designated an individual or individuals responsible for the overall BSA/AML compliance program who are appropriately qualified.”
shouldexaminersreview reports to the board of directors and senior management regarding the status of ongoing compliance and pertinent BSA-related information, including the required notification of SAR filings
✓ “Examiners should review reports to the board of directors and senior management regarding the status of ongoing compliance and pertinent BSA-related information, including the required notification of SAR filings.”
shouldexaminersconfirm that the BSA compliance officer has the appropriate authority, independence, and access to resources
✓ “Examiners should confirm that the BSA compliance officer has the appropriate authority, independence, and access to resources.”

BSA/AML Training · AssessingTheBSAAMLComplianceProgram/05
Actor / modalityObligation
mustBanksprovide training for appropriate personnel
✓ “Banks must provide training for appropriate personnel.”
shouldBank (training program)cover the aspects of the BSA that are relevant to the bank and its risk profile, including BSA regulatory requirements, supervisory guidance, and the bank's internal BSA/AML policies, procedures, and processes
✓ “Training should cover the aspects of the BSA that are relevant to the bank and its risk profile, and appropriate personnel includes those whose duties require knowledge or involve some aspect of BSA/AML compliance. Training should cover BSA regulatory requirements, supervisory guidance, and the bank's internal BSA/AML policies, procedures, and processes.”
shouldBank (training program)tailor training to each individual's specific responsibilities, as appropriate
exceptions: as appropriate
✓ “Training should be tailored to each individual's specific responsibilities, as appropriate.”
mayBankprovide targeted training for specific ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks and requirements applicable to certain business lines or operational units

✓ “targeted training may be necessary for specific ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks and requirements applicable to certain business lines or operational units, such as lending, trust services, foreign correspondent banking, and private banking.”
shouldBSA compliance officer and BSA compliance staffreceive periodic training that is relevant and appropriate to remain informed of changes to regulatory requirements and changes to the bank's risk profile
✓ “The BSA compliance officer and BSA compliance staff should receive periodic training that is relevant and appropriate to remain informed of changes to regulatory requirements and changes to the bank's risk profile.”

✓ “The board of directors and senior management should receive foundational training and be informed of changes and new developments in the BSA, including its implementing regulations, the federal banking agencies' regulations, and supervisory guidance.”
shouldBoard trainingprovide board members with sufficient understanding of the bank's risk profile and BSA regulatory requirements
exceptions: board of directors may not require the same degree of training as banking operations personnel
✓ “While the board of directors may not require the same degree of training as banking operations personnel, the training should provide board members with sufficient understanding of the bank's risk profile and BSA regulatory requirements.”
shouldPeriodic training for appropriate personnelincorporate current developments and changes to BSA regulatory requirements; supervisory guidance; internal policies, procedures, and processes; and the bank's products, services, customers, and geographic locations
✓ “Periodic training for appropriate personnel should incorporate current developments and changes to BSA regulatory requirements; supervisory guidance; internal policies, procedures, and processes; and the bank's products, services, customers, and geographic locations.”
mayBank (training on IT changes)cover changes to information technology sources, systems, and processes used in BSA compliance during training for appropriate personnel
✓ “Changes to information technology sources, systems, and processes used in BSA compliance may be covered during training for appropriate personnel.”
mayBank (training program)be used to reinforce the importance that the board of directors and senior management place on the bank's compliance with the BSA

shouldTraining programsinclude examples of money laundering and suspicious activity monitoring and reporting that are tailored, as appropriate, to each operational area
exceptions: as appropriate
✓ “Training programs should include examples of money laundering and suspicious activity monitoring and reporting that are tailored, as appropriate, to each operational area.”

trigger: If the bank relies on another financial institution or other party to perform training
✓ “If the bank relies on another financial institution or other party to perform training, appropriate documentation should be maintained.”
shouldBanksdocument their training programs
✓ “Banks should document their training programs.”
conditionalBankmaintain training and testing materials (if training-related testing is used by the bank), and the dates of training sessions
trigger: if training-related testing is used by the bank
✓ “Training and testing materials (if training-related testing is used by the bank), and the dates of training sessions should be maintained by the bank.”
shouldBank (training materials and records)be available for auditor or examiner review
✓ “Additionally, training materials and records should be available for auditor or examiner review.”
shouldBankmaintain documentation of attendance records and any failures of personnel to take the required training in a timely manner, as well as any corrective actions taken to address such failures
✓ “The bank should maintain documentation of attendance records and any failures of personnel to take the required training in a timely manner, as well as any corrective actions taken to address such failures.”

ffiec-man-ps-01

BSA/AML Compliance Program Structures · ProgramStructures/01
Actor / modalityObligation
mustEvery bankhave a comprehensive BSA/AML compliance program that addresses BSA requirements applicable to all operations of the organization
✓ “Every bank must have a comprehensive BSA/AML compliance program that addresses BSA requirements applicable to all operations of the organization.”
mayA banking organizationstructure and manage the BSA/AML compliance program or some parts of the program within a legal entity; with some degree of consolidation across entities; or as part of a comprehensive enterprise risk management framework
✓ “A banking organization may structure and manage the BSA/AML compliance program or some parts of the program within a legal entity; with some degree of consolidation across entities within an organization; or as part of a comprehensive enterprise risk management framework.”
shouldConsolidated BSA/AML compliance programreflect the organization's business structure, size, and complexity, and be designed to effectively address risks, exposures, and applicable legal requirements across the organization
trigger: Regardless of how a consolidated BSA/AML compliance program is organized
✓ “Regardless of how a consolidated BSA/AML compliance program is organized, it should reflect the organization's business structure, size, and complexity, and be designed to effectively address risks, exposures, and applicable legal requirements across the organization.”
shouldA consolidated approachinclude the establishment of corporate standards for BSA/AML compliance that reflect the expectations of the organization's board of directors
✓ “A consolidated approach should also include the establishment of corporate standards for BSA/AML compliance that reflect the expectations of the organization's board of directors, with senior management working to ensure that the BSA/AML compliance program implements these corporate standards.”
shouldSenior management (under consolidated approach)work to ensure that the BSA/AML compliance program implements the corporate standards
✓ “with senior management working to ensure that the BSA/AML compliance program implements these corporate standards”
shouldRisk (under a consolidated approach)be assessed both within and across all business lines, legal entities, and jurisdictions of operation
trigger: Under a consolidated approach
✓ “Under a consolidated approach, risk should be assessed both within and across all business lines, legal entities, and jurisdictions of operation.”
shouldPrograms for global organizationsincorporate the AML laws and requirements of the various jurisdictions in which they operate
✓ “Programs for global organizations should incorporate the AML laws and requirements of the various jurisdictions in which they operate.”
shouldInternal auditassess the level of compliance with the consolidated BSA/AML compliance program
✓ “Internal audit should assess the level of compliance with the consolidated BSA/AML compliance program.”
shouldExaminerscoordinate efforts with other regulatory agencies in order to address these challenges or ensure the examination scope appropriately covers the legal entity examined
trigger: As appropriate, when a diversified banking organization uses multiple legal entities examined by multiple regulators
✓ “As appropriate, examiners should coordinate efforts with other regulatory agencies in order to address these challenges or ensure the examination scope appropriately covers the legal entity examined.”
shouldSmall institution combining BSA/AML compliance with other functionsthere should still be adequate senior-level attention to BSA/AML compliance, and sufficient dedicated resources
trigger: In such circumstances (small institution combining BSA/AML compliance with other functions)
✓ “In such circumstances, there should still be adequate senior-level attention to BSA/AML compliance, and sufficient dedicated resources.”
shouldThe audit functionremain independent
✓ “As is the case in all structures, the audit function should remain independent.”
shouldBanking organization (when BSA/AML compliance responsibilities are delegated and staff located within lines of business)clearly set forth expectations in order to ensure effective implementation of the BSA/AML compliance program
trigger: when there is delegation of BSA/AML compliance responsibilities, and BSA/AML compliance staff is located within lines of business
✓ “when there is delegation of BSA/AML compliance responsibilities, and BSA/AML compliance staff is located within lines of business, expectations should be clearly set forth in order to ensure effective implementation of the BSA/AML compliance program”
shouldAllocation of responsibilitybe clear with respect to the content and comprehensiveness of MIS reports, the depth and frequency of monitoring efforts, and the role of different parties within the banking organization in BSA/AML compliance decision-making processes
✓ “In particular, allocation of responsibility should be clear with respect to the content and comprehensiveness of MIS reports, the depth and frequency of monitoring efforts, and the role of different parties within the banking organization (e.g., risk, business lines, operations) in BSA/AML compliance decision-making processes.”
shouldAn appropriate level of BSA/AML compliance independencebe maintained (e.g., by providing a reporting line to corporate compliance; ensuring active involvement in all matters affecting AML risk; establishing a dispute escalation/resolution process; establishing internal controls to maintain objectivity)
trigger: when compliance staff reports to the management of the business line, presenting risks of potential conflicts of interest
✓ “To ensure the strength of compliance controls, an appropriate level of BSA/AML compliance independence should be maintained, for example, by:”
mustThe board of directorshas primary responsibility for ensuring that the bank has a comprehensive and effective BSA/AML compliance program and oversight framework reasonably designed to ensure compliance with BSA/AML regulation
✓ “The board of directors has primary responsibility for ensuring that the bank has a comprehensive and effective BSA/AML compliance program and oversight framework that is reasonably designed to ensure compliance with BSA/AML regulation.”
mustSenior managementis responsible for implementing the board-approved BSA/AML compliance program
✓ “Senior management is responsible for implementing the board-approved BSA/AML compliance program.”
mustThe board of directorsis responsible for approving the BSA/AML compliance program and for overseeing the structure and management of the bank's BSA/AML compliance function
✓ “The board of directors is responsible for approving the BSA/AML compliance program and for overseeing the structure and management of the bank's BSA/AML compliance function.”
shouldThe boardensure that senior management is fully capable, qualified, and properly motivated to manage the BSA/AML compliance risks in a manner consistent with the board's expectations
✓ “The board should ensure that senior management is fully capable, qualified, and properly motivated to manage the BSA/AML compliance risks arising from the organization's business activities in a manner that is consistent with the board's expectations.”
shouldThe boardensure that the BSA/AML compliance function has an appropriately prominent status within the organization
✓ “The board should ensure that the BSA/AML compliance function has an appropriately prominent status within the organization.”
shouldThe boardensure that its views about the importance of BSA/AML compliance are understood and communicated across all levels of the banking organization
✓ “The board should ensure that its views about the importance of BSA/AML compliance are understood and communicated across all levels of the banking organization.”
shouldThe boardensure that senior management has established appropriate incentives to integrate BSA/AML compliance objectives into management goals and compensation structure, and that corrective actions, including disciplinary measures if appropriate, are taken when serious BSA/AML compliance failures are identified
✓ “The board also should ensure that senior management has established appropriate incentives to integrate BSA/AML compliance objectives into management goals and compensation structure across the organization, and that corrective actions, including disciplinary measures, if appropriate, are taken when serious BSA/AML compliance failures are identified.”
mustSenior managementis responsible for communicating and reinforcing the BSA/AML compliance culture established by the board, and implementing and enforcing the board-approved BSA/AML compliance program
✓ “Senior management is responsible for communicating and reinforcing the BSA/AML compliance culture established by the board, and implementing and enforcing the board-approved BSA/AML compliance program.”
shouldSenior management of the BSA/AML compliance functionestablish, support, and oversee the organization's BSA/AML compliance program
trigger: If the banking organization has a separate BSA/AML compliance function
✓ “If the banking organization has a separate BSA/AML compliance function, senior management of the function should establish, support, and oversee the organization's BSA/AML compliance program.”
shouldBSA/AML compliance staffreport to the board, or a committee thereof, on the effectiveness of the BSA/AML compliance program and significant BSA/AML compliance matters
✓ “BSA/AML compliance staff should report to the board, or a committee thereof, on the effectiveness of the BSA/AML compliance program and significant BSA/AML compliance matters.”
shouldSenior management of a foreign banking organization's U.S. operationsprovide sufficient information relating to the U.S. operations' BSA/AML compliance to the governance or control functions in its home country, and ensure that responsible senior management in the home country has an appropriate understanding of the BSA/AML risk and control environment governing U.S. operations
✓ “Senior management of a foreign banking organization's U.S. operations should provide sufficient information relating to the U.S. operations' BSA/AML compliance to the governance or control functions in its home country, and should ensure that responsible senior management in the home country has an appropriate understanding of the BSA/AML risk and control environment governing U.S. operations.”
shouldU.S. management (of a foreign banking organization's U.S. operations)assess the effectiveness of established BSA/AML control mechanisms for U.S. operations on an ongoing basis and report and escalate areas of concern as needed
✓ “U.S. management should assess the effectiveness of established BSA/AML control mechanisms for U.S. operations on an ongoing basis and report and escalate areas of concern as needed.”
shouldBanking organizations that centrally manage the operations and functions of their subsidiary banks, other subsidiaries, and business linesensure that comprehensive risk management policies, procedures, and processes are in place across the organization to address the entire organization's spectrum of risk
✓ “Banking organizations that centrally manage the operations and functions of their subsidiary banks, other subsidiaries, and business lines should ensure that comprehensive risk management policies, procedures, and processes are in place across the organization to address the entire organization's spectrum of risk.”
shouldBanking organizations that centrally manage a consolidated BSA/AML compliance programamong other things provide appropriate structure; and advise the business lines, subsidiaries, and foreign branches on the development of appropriate guidelines
✓ “banking organizations that centrally manage a consolidated BSA/AML compliance program should, among other things provide appropriate structure; and advise the business lines, subsidiaries, and foreign branches on the development of appropriate guidelines”
mustExaminersidentify which portions of the BSA/AML compliance program are part of the consolidated BSA/AML compliance program
trigger: When an organization manages only specific compliance controls on a consolidated basis with others managed within affiliates, subsidiaries, and business lines
✓ “When this approach is taken, examiners must identify which portions of the BSA/AML compliance program are part of the consolidated BSA/AML compliance program.”
shouldThe examinerdetermine reporting lines and how each affiliate, subsidiary, business line, and jurisdiction fits into the overall compliance structure, including an assessment of how clearly roles and responsibilities are communicated
trigger: When evaluating a consolidated BSA/AML compliance program for adequacy
✓ “When evaluating a consolidated BSA/AML compliance program for adequacy, the examiner should determine reporting lines and how each affiliate, subsidiary, business line, and jurisdiction fits into the overall compliance structure.”
shouldThe examinerassess how effectively the bank or banking organization monitors BSA/AML compliance throughout the organization, including how well the consolidated and nonconsolidated program captures relevant data from subsidiaries
✓ “The examiner also should assess how effectively the bank or banking organization monitors BSA/AML compliance throughout the organization, including how well the consolidated and nonconsolidated BSA/AML compliance program captures relevant data from subsidiaries.”
shouldThe evaluation of a consolidated BSA/AML compliance programtake into consideration available information about the adequacy of the individual subsidiaries' BSA/AML compliance program
✓ “The evaluation of a consolidated BSA/AML compliance program should take into consideration available information about the adequacy of the individual subsidiaries' BSA/AML compliance program.”
shouldThe (consolidated) programensure that all affiliates, including those operating within foreign jurisdictions, meet their applicable regulatory requirements
trigger: Regardless of the decision to implement a consolidated BSA/AML compliance program in whole or in part
✓ “Regardless of the decision to implement a consolidated BSA/AML compliance program in whole or in part, the program should ensure that all affiliates, including those operating within foreign jurisdictions, meet their applicable regulatory requirements.”
shouldAuditbe aware and ensure AML controls are commensurate with those risks
trigger: If dissemination of certain information is limited and therefore transparency across the organization is restricted
✓ “If dissemination of certain information is limited and therefore transparency across the organization is restricted, audit should be aware and ensure AML controls are commensurate with those risks.”
mustBank holding companies (BHC) or any nonbank subsidiary thereof, or a foreign bank subject to the BHC Act or any nonbank subsidiary of such a foreign bank operating in the United Statesfile SARs
exceptions: A BHC's nonbank subsidiaries operating only outside the United States are not required to file SARs
✓ “Bank holding companies (BHC) or any nonbank subsidiary thereof, or a foreign bank that is subject to the BHC Act or any nonbank subsidiary of such a foreign bank operating in the United States, are required to file SARs.”
✓ “A BHC's nonbank subsidiaries operating only outside the United States, are not required to file SARs.”
mustCertain savings and loan holding companies, and their nondepository subsidiariesfile SARs pursuant to Treasury regulations (e.g., insurance companies (31 CFR 1025.320) and broker/dealers (31 CFR 1023.320))
✓ “Certain savings and loan holding companies, and their nondepository subsidiaries, are required to file SARs pursuant to Treasury regulations (e.g., insurance companies (31 CFR 1025.320) and broker/dealers (31 CFR 1023.320)).”
shouldSavings and loan holding companies (if not required)are strongly encouraged to file SARs in appropriate circumstances
trigger: if not required
✓ “In addition, savings and loan holding companies, if not required, are strongly encouraged to file SARs in appropriate circumstances.”

ffiec-man-ra

BSA/AML Risk Assessment · BSAAMLRiskAssessment/01
Actor / modalityObligation
mustExaminersdevelop an understanding of the bank's ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks to evaluate the bank's BSA/AML compliance program
trigger: evaluating the bank's BSA/AML compliance program
✓ “Examiners must develop an understanding of the bank's ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks to evaluate the bank's BSA/AML compliance program.”
shouldThe bank's BSA/AML risk assessment processaddress the varying degrees of risk associated with its products, services, customers, and geographic locations, as appropriate
exceptions: as appropriate
✓ “The bank's BSA/AML risk assessment process should address the varying degrees of risk associated with its products, services, customers, and geographic locations, as appropriate.”
shouldThe BSA/AML risk assessmentprovide a comprehensive analysis of the bank's ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks
✓ “The BSA/AML risk assessment should provide a comprehensive analysis of the bank's ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks.”
shouldThe BSA/AML risk assessmentbe provided to all business lines across the bank, the board of directors, management, and appropriate staff
✓ “The BSA/AML risk assessment should be provided to all business lines across the bank, the board of directors, management, and appropriate staff.”
shouldA conclusion regarding the risk categoriesbe based on a consideration of all pertinent information
✓ “The identification of risk categories is bank-specific, and a conclusion regarding the risk categories should be based on a consideration of all pertinent information.”
shouldExaminersnot criticize the bank for individual risk or process decisions unless those decisions impact the adequacy of some aspect of the bank's BSA/AML compliance program or the bank's compliance with BSA regulatory requirements
trigger: when the bank has established an appropriate BSA/AML risk assessment process, and has followed existing policies, procedures, and processes
exceptions: unless those decisions impact the adequacy of some aspect of the bank's BSA/AML compliance program or the bank's compliance with BSA regulatory requirements
✓ “When the bank has established an appropriate BSA/AML risk assessment process, and has followed existing policies, procedures, and processes, examiners should not criticize the bank for individual risk or process decisions unless those decisions impact the adequacy of some aspect of the bank's BSA/AML compliance program or the bank's compliance with BSA regulatory requirements.”
shouldExaminersfocus on whether the bank has effective processes resulting in a well-developed BSA/AML risk assessment
trigger: when evaluating the BSA/AML risk assessment
✓ “When evaluating the BSA/AML risk assessment, examiners should focus on whether the bank has effective processes resulting in a well-developed BSA/AML risk assessment.”
shouldExaminersnot take any single indicator as determinative of the existence of a lower- or higher-risk profile for the bank
✓ “Examiners should not take any single indicator as determinative of the existence of a lower- or higher-risk profile for the bank.”
shouldA conclusion regarding the risk profilebe based on a consideration of all pertinent information
✓ “The assessment of risk factors is bank-specific, and a conclusion regarding the risk profile should be based on a consideration of all pertinent information.”
shouldExaminersassess whether the bank has developed a BSA/AML risk assessment that identifies its ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks
✓ “Examiners should assess whether the bank has developed a BSA/AML risk assessment that identifies its ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks.”
shouldExaminersassess whether the bank has considered all products, services, customers, and geographic locations, and whether the bank analyzed the information relative to those risk categories
✓ “Examiners should also assess whether the bank has considered all products, services, customers, and geographic locations, and whether the bank analyzed the information relative to those risk categories.”
mustExaminersdevelop a BSA/AML risk assessment for the bank based on available information
trigger: whenever the bank has not developed a BSA/AML risk assessment, or the BSA/AML risk assessment is inadequate
✓ “For the purposes of the examination, whenever the bank has not developed a BSA/AML risk assessment, or the BSA/AML risk assessment is inadequate, examiners must develop a BSA/AML risk assessment for the bank based on available information.”
shouldThe bankdevelop appropriate policies, procedures, and processes to monitor and control its ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks
✓ “Specifically, the bank should develop appropriate policies, procedures, and processes to monitor and control its ML/TF and other illicit financial activity risks.”

shouldthe reviewprovide a fair and unbiased appraisal of each of the required elements of the company's anti-money laundering program, including its Bank Secrecy Act-related policies, procedures, internal controls, recordkeeping and reporting functions, and training
✓ “The review should provide a fair and unbiased appraisal of each of the required elements of the company’s anti-money laundering program, including its Bank Secrecy Act-related policies, procedures, internal controls, recordkeeping and reporting functions, and training.”
shouldthe reviewinclude testing of internal controls and transactional systems and procedures to identify problems and weaknesses and, if necessary, recommend to management appropriate corrective actions
✓ “The review should include testing of internal controls and transactional systems and procedures to identify problems and weaknesses and, if necessary, recommend to management appropriate corrective actions.”
shouldthe reviewcover all of the anti-money laundering program actions taken by - or defined as part of the responsibility of - the designated compliance officer
✓ “The review also should cover all of the anti-money laundering program actions taken by - or defined as part of the responsibility of - the designated compliance officer.”
must_notthe review (reviewer)be conducted by the designated compliance officer or someone who reports directly to the compliance officer
✓ “The review may be conducted by an officer, employee or group of employees, so long as the reviewer is not the designated compliance officer and does not report directly to the compliance officer.”
shouldthe reviewbe conducted on a periodic basis
threshold: The scope and frequency of the review will depend on the money services business' risk assessment
✓ “The review should be conducted on a periodic basis.”
✓ “The scope and frequency of the review will depend on the money services business’ risk assessment, which should take into account the business’ products, services, customers, and geographic locations.”
shouldthe person or persons responsible for conducting the reviewdocument the scope of the review, procedures performed, transaction testing completed, if any, findings of the review, and recommendations to management for corrective actions, if any
✓ “The person or persons responsible for conducting the review should document the scope of the review, procedures performed, transaction testing completed, if any, findings of the review, and recommendations to management for corrective actions, if any.”
shouldthe reviewer or the designated compliance officertrack deficiencies and weaknesses discovered during the review and document corrective actions taken by the money services business
trigger: After the review
✓ “After the review, the reviewer or the designated compliance officer should track deficiencies and weaknesses discovered during the review and document corrective actions taken by the money services business.”
shouldthe documentation (money services business)be made accessible to government examiners and law enforcement personnel who have authority to examine such documents
exceptions: as appropriate
✓ “All of the documentation should, as appropriate, be made accessible to government examiners and law enforcement personnel who have authority to examine such documents.”
mustmoney services businessesestablish anti-money laundering programs tailored to their operations and the money laundering risks posed
✓ “Our regulations at 31 C.F.R. § 103.125 require money services businesses to establish anti-money laundering programs tailored to their operations and the money laundering risks posed.”

fincen-guid-existing-program-rule-obligations

Guidance on Existing AML Program Rule Compliance Obligations for MSB Principals with Respect to Agent Monitoring · https://www.fincen.gov/resources/statutes-regulations/guidance/guidance-existing-aml-program-rule-compliance-obligations
Actor / modalityObligation
mustall MSBs, both principals and their agentsestablish and maintain an effective written AML program reasonably designed to prevent the MSB from being used to facilitate money laundering and the financing of terrorist activities
✓ “The Bank Secrecy Act (BSA) requires all MSBs, both principals and their agents, to establish and maintain an effective written AML program reasonably designed to prevent the MSB from being used to facilitate money laundering and the financing of terrorist activities.”
musteach MSBremain independently and wholly responsible for implementing adequate AML program requirements; cannot avoid liability by pointing to a contract assigning this responsibility to another party
✓ “Moreover, each MSB remains independently and wholly responsible for implementing adequate AML program requirements.7 Accordingly, neither the agent nor the principal can avoid liability for failing to establish and maintain an effective AML program by pointing to a contract assigning this responsibility to another party (whether the agent or principal).”
mustan MSB's AML programat a minimum: incorporate policies, procedures, and internal controls reasonably designed to assure compliance with the BSA and its implementing regulations
threshold: at a minimum
✓ “Under 31 CFR § 1022.210, an MSB’s AML program must, at a minimum:”
✓ “Incorporate policies, procedures, and internal controls reasonably designed to assure compliance with the BSA and its implementing regulations”
mustan MSB's AML programdesignate a person to assure day to day compliance with the program and the BSA and its implementing regulations
threshold: at a minimum
✓ “Designate a person to assure day to day compliance with the program and the BSA and its implementing regulations”
mustan MSB's AML programprovide education and training of appropriate personnel concerning their responsibilities under the program, including training in the detection of suspicious transactions to the extent that the MSB is required to report such transactions under the BSA
threshold: at a minimum
✓ “Provide education and training of appropriate personnel concerning their responsibilities under the program, including training in the detection of suspicious transactions to the extent that the money services business is required to report such transactions under the BSA”
mustan MSB's AML programprovide for independent review to monitor and maintain an adequate program
threshold: at a minimum
✓ “Provide for independent review to monitor and maintain an adequate program”
mustthe MSB principalhave procedures in place to identify those agents conducting activities that appear to lack commercial purpose, lack justification, or otherwise are not supported by verifiable documentation
trigger: in order to reduce exposure to risks when an agent engages in transactions that create a risk for money laundering, terrorist financing, or other financial crime
✓ “the MSB principal must have procedures in place to identify those agents conducting activities that appear to lack commercial purpose, lack justification, or otherwise are not supported by verifiable documentation.”
mustthe principalimplement risk-based procedures to monitor the agents' transactions to ensure that they are legitimate
✓ “The principal must implement risk-based procedures to monitor the agents’ transactions to ensure that they are legitimate.”
mustthe principal (procedures)ensure that, if the agents' transactions trigger reporting or recordkeeping requirements, the principal handles the information in accordance with regulatory reporting and recordkeeping obligations
trigger: if the agents' transactions trigger reporting or recordkeeping requirements
✓ “The procedures must also ensure that, if the agents’ transactions trigger reporting or recordkeeping requirements, the principal handles the information in accordance with regulatory reporting and recordkeeping obligations.”
shouldthe MSB principalimplement procedures for handling non-compliant agents, including agent contract terminations
✓ “the MSB principal should implement procedures for handling non-compliant agents, including agent contract terminations.”
mustprincipalsdevelop and implement risk-based policies, procedures, and internal controls that ensure adequate ongoing monitoring of agent activity, as part of the principal's implementation of its AML program
✓ “principals are required to develop and implement risk-based policies, procedures, and internal controls that ensure adequate ongoing monitoring of agent activity, as part of the principal’s implementation of its AML program.”
mustprincipalswhen conducting monitoring of their agents, at a minimum: identify the owners of the MSB's agents
trigger: when conducting monitoring of their agents
threshold: at a minimum
✓ “When conducting monitoring of their agents, principals must, at a minimum:”
✓ “Identify the owners of the MSB’s agents”
mustprincipalswhen conducting monitoring of their agents, evaluate on an ongoing basis the operations of agents, and monitor for variations in those operations
trigger: when conducting monitoring of their agents
threshold: at a minimum
✓ “Evaluate on an ongoing basis the operations of agents, and monitor for variations in those operations”
mustprincipalswhen conducting monitoring of their agents, evaluate agents' implementation of policies, procedures, and controls
trigger: when conducting monitoring of their agents
threshold: at a minimum
✓ “Evaluate agents’ implementation of policies, procedures, and controls”
mustprincipalsperiodically reassess risks associated with their agents and update the principals' programs to address any changing or additional related risks
✓ “Principals must periodically reassess risks associated with their agents and update the principals’ programs to address any changing or additional related risks.”
mustprincipalstake corrective action once becoming aware of any weaknesses or deficiencies in their AML programs
trigger: once becoming aware of any weaknesses or deficiencies in their AML programs
✓ “Principals must also take corrective action once becoming aware of any weaknesses or deficiencies in their AML programs.”
mustMSB principals and agentsconduct reviews with a scope and frequency commensurate with the risks of money laundering or other illicit activity such principal or agent faces
threshold: scope and frequency commensurate with the risks of money laundering or other illicit activity
✓ “MSB principals and agents are required to conduct reviews with a scope and frequency commensurate with the risks of money laundering or other illicit activity such principal or agent faces.”
musta principalconduct internal and/or external independent testing to ensure there are no material weaknesses or internal control deficiencies; the testing must factor in products and services provided to determine if the procedures are sufficient to detect and report suspicious activity
✓ “A principal must conduct internal and/or external independent testing to ensure there are no material weaknesses (e.g., inadequate training) or internal control deficiencies (e.g., monitoring agents).”
✓ “In addition, the testing must factor in products and services provided to determine if the procedures are sufficient to detect and report suspicious activity.”
shouldprincipalsconsider risk factors when conducting agent monitoring, including but not limited to whether owners are known or suspected associated with criminal conduct or terrorism, whether the agent has an established AML program, the nature of markets served, services expected and anticipated activity level, and the nature and duration of the relationship
trigger: when conducting agent monitoring
✓ “Risk factors that principals should consider when conducting agent monitoring include, but are not limited to:”
✓ “Whether the owners are known or suspected to be associated with criminal conduct or terrorism”
✓ “The services an agent is expected to provide and the agent’s anticipated level of activity”
mustprincipals (with agents providing services involving regions affected by conflict or terrorism)take steps to account for and mitigate such risks
trigger: agents providing services involving regions affected by conflict or terrorism
✓ “This does not mean that principals with agents providing services involving regions affected by conflict or terrorism cannot manage such risks, but rather that principals must take steps to account for and mitigate such risks”
shouldMSB principals and agents (as expected by FinCEN)tailor their AML programs to reflect the risks associated with their particular business services, clients, size, locations, and circumstances
✓ “FinCEN expects MSB principals and agents to tailor their AML programs to reflect the risks associated with their particular business services, clients, size, locations, and circumstances.”
conditionalprincipalsencouraged to share information with other eligible financial institutions through participation in the 314(b) voluntary information-sharing program where an agent has more than one principal or provides additional services on its own
trigger: an agent may enter into contracts to offer services with more than one principal or may provide additional services on its own
✓ “In such situations, principals are encouraged to share information with other eligible financial institutions through participation in the 314(b) voluntary information-sharing program.”
TermDefinition (as given)
agentFor purposes of this guidance includes authorized delegates, foreign agents or counterparties, agents and sub-agents.
✓ “The term “agent” for purposes of this guidance includes authorized delegates, foreign agents or counterparties, agents and sub-agents.”

fincen-ir-2004-1-aml-program

Guidance - (Interpretive Release 2004-1) Anti-Money Laundering Program - Requirements for Money Services Businesses With Respect to Foreign Agents or Foreign Counterparties · https://www.fincen.gov/resources/statutes-regulations/guidance/guidance-interpretive-release-2004-1-anti-money-laundering
Actor / modalityObligation
mustMoney Services Businesses required to register with FinCENdevelop, implement, and maintain an effective anti-money laundering program reasonably designed to prevent the MSB from being used to facilitate money laundering and the financing of terrorist activities
trigger: MSB status under 31 CFR 103.125(a)
✗ “Money Services Businesses are required to develop, implement, and maintain an effective anti-money laundering program reasonably designed to prevent the Money Services Business from being used to facilitate money laundering and the financing of terrorist activities.”
mustMoney Services Businessmake the program commensurate with the risks posed by the location, size, nature, and volume of the financial services provided
✓ “The program must be commensurate with the risks posed by the location, size, nature, and volume of the financial services provided by the Money Services Business.”
mustMoney Services Businessincorporate policies, procedures, and controls reasonably designed to assure compliance with the Bank Secrecy Act and implementing regulations
✓ “the program must incorporate policies, procedures, and controls reasonably designed to assure compliance with the Bank Secrecy Act and implementing regulations.”
mustMoney Services Business that utilizes foreign agents or counterpartiesinclude in its AML program risk-based policies, procedures, and controls designed to identify and minimize money laundering and terrorist financing risks associated with foreign agents and counterparties that facilitate the flow of funds into and out of the United States
trigger: MSB utilizes foreign agents or foreign counterparties facilitating cross-border flow of funds
✓ “a Money Services Business’ anti-money laundering program must include risk-based policies, procedures, and controls designed to identify and minimize money laundering and terrorist financing risks associated with foreign agents and counterparties that facilitate the flow of funds into and out of the United States.”
mustMoney Services Businessaim the program at preventing its products and services from being used to facilitate money laundering or terrorist financing through foreign agent/counterparty relationships and at detecting such use
✗ “The program must be aimed at preventing the products and services of the Money Services Business from being used to facilitate money laundering or terrorist financing through these relationships and detecting the use of these products and services for money laundering or terrorist financing by the Money Services Business or agent.”
mustMoney Services Businessconduct due diligence, at a minimum including reasonable procedures to identify the owners of its foreign agents and counterparties and to evaluate on an ongoing basis their operations and implementation of policies, procedures, and controls
trigger: MSB has potential or existing foreign agents or counterparties
exceptions: May allocate responsibility for the program to domestic agents under 31 CFR 103.125(d)(iii), which does not relieve the MSB of ultimate responsibility
✓ “Such due diligence must, at a minimum, include reasonable procedures to identify the owners of the Money Services Business’ foreign agents and counterparties, as well as to evaluate, on an ongoing basis, the operations of those foreign agents and counterparties and their implementation of policies, procedures, and controls reasonably designed to help assure that the Money Services Business’ products and services are not subject to abuse by the foreign agent’s or counterparty’s customers, employees, or contractors.”
shouldMoney Services Businessestablish procedures for conducting reasonable, risk-based due diligence on potential and existing foreign agents and counterparties
✓ “Money Services Businesses should establish procedures for conducting reasonable, risk-based due diligence on potential and existing foreign agents and counterparties to help ensure that such foreign agents and counterparties are not themselves complicit in illegal activity involving the Money Services Business’ products and services”
shouldMoney Services Businessestablish procedures for risk-based monitoring and review of transactions from, to, or through the United States that are conducted through foreign agents and counterparties
✓ “Money Services Businesses should establish procedures for risk-based monitoring and review of transactions from, to, or through the United States that are conducted through foreign agents and counterparties.”
shouldMoney Services Businessfocus monitoring procedures on identifying material changes in the agent's risk profile, such as a change in ownership, business, or the regulatory scrutiny to which it is subject
✓ “Such procedures should also focus on identifying material changes in the agent’s risk profile, such as a change in ownership, business, or the regulatory scrutiny to which it is subject.”
shouldMoney Services Businessestablish procedures to assure foreign agents or counterparties are effectively implementing an anti-money laundering program and to discern obvious breakdowns in implementation
✓ “Money Services Businesses should establish procedures to assure that their foreign agents or counterparties are effectively implementing an anti-money laundering program and to discern obvious breakdowns in the implementation of the program by the foreign agent or counterparty.”
shouldmoney transmittershave procedures in place to review foreign agent or counterparty activity for signs of structuring or unnecessarily complex transmissions through multiple jurisdictions that may be indicative of layering, and to discern attempts to evade identification or other requirements
✓ “money transmitters should have procedures in place to enable them to review foreign agent or counterparty activity for signs of structuring or unnecessarily complex transmissions through multiple jurisdictions that may be indicative of layering.”
mustMoney Services Businessscrutinize activity by agents or counterparties that appears aimed at evading the MSB's own controls, report as appropriate, and take corrective action as warranted
trigger: activity by agents or counterparties appears aimed at evading MSB's own controls
✓ “this activity must be scrutinized, reported as appropriate, and corrective action taken as warranted.”
shouldMoney Services Businesshave procedures for responding to foreign agents or counterparties that present unreasonable risks of money laundering or the financing of terrorism, providing for corrective action or termination of the relationship
trigger: foreign agent/counterparty poses unacceptable risk of ML/TF or has demonstrated systemic, willful, or repeated lapses in compliance
✗ “Such procedures should provide for the implementation of corrective action on the part of the foreign agent or counterparty or for the termination of the relationship with any foreign agent or counterparty that the Money Services Business determines poses an unacceptable risk of money laundering or terrorist financing, or that has demonstrated systemic, willful, or repeated lapses in compliance with the Money Services Business’ own anti-money laundering procedures or requirements.”
mustaffected Money Services Businessesachieve full compliance with this Interpretive Release
threshold: within 180 days
✓ “We will expect full compliance with this Interpretive Release within 180 days.”
TermDefinition (as given)
Money Services BusinessUnder existing Bank Secrecy Act regulations, defined to include five distinct types of financial services providers and the U.S. Postal Service: (1) Currency dealers or exchangers; (2) check cashers; (3) issuers of traveler’s checks, money orders, or stored value; (4) sellers or redeemers of traveler’s checks, money orders, or stored value; and (5) money transmitters.
✗ “we have defined Money Services Businesses to include five distinct types of financial services providers and the U.S. Postal Service: (1) Currency dealers or exchangers; (2) check cashers; (3) issuers of traveler’s checks, money orders, or stored value; (4) sellers or redeemers of traveler’s checks, money orders, or stored value; and (5) money transmitters.”
foreign agent or foreign counterparty(undefined here)
✓ “the establishment of, and ongoing relationship between, a Money Services Business and its foreign agent or foreign counterparty that facilitates the flow of funds cross-border into and out of the United States on behalf of customers.”
anti-money laundering programAn effective program, reasonably designed to prevent the Money Services Business from being used to facilitate money laundering and the financing of terrorist activities, required under 31 CFR 103.125(a), commensurate with risks posed by location, size, nature, and volume of financial services provided, incorporating policies, procedures, and controls reasonably designed to assure compliance with the Bank Secrecy Act and implementing regulations.
✗ “Money Services Businesses are required to develop, implement, and maintain an effective anti-money laundering program reasonably designed to prevent the Money Services Business from being used to facilitate money laundering and the financing of terrorist activities.”
hawala system(undefined here)
✓ “Other Money Services Businesses may operate through more informal relationships, such as the trust-based hawala system.”
layering(undefined here)
✓ “unnecessarily complex transmissions through multiple jurisdictions that may be indicative of layering.”

Notes: This item is a FinCEN interpretive release (Appendix C to 31 CFR Part 103) rather than a standalone regulation; it interprets the pre-existing 31 CFR 103.125 AML program rule as applied specifically to MSB foreign agent/counterparty relationships. The text uses 'must' in the operative program-elements section but 'should' throughout the specific procedural recommendations (due diligence, monitoring, corrective action/termination), preserved here as written despite the apparent modality shift between mandatory program content and recommended implementing procedures.
